## 1. Robust Certification Of Non-Projective Measurements: Theory And Experiment

- **Score**: 9.3 / 10  |  **Recommended**: ✅ Yes
- **Source**: PRX Quantum  |  **Feed**: PRX Quantum
- **Direction**: Quantum Information
- **Published**: 2026-07-06
- **Authors**: Raphael Brinster, Peter Tirler, Shishir Khandelwal, Michael Meth, Hermann Kampermann, Dagmar Bruß, Rainer Blatt, Martin Ringbauer, Armin Tavakoli, And Nikolai Wyderka
- **Link**: [http://link.aps.org/doi/10.1103/nsjr-vnmg](http://link.aps.org/doi/10.1103/nsjr-vnmg)
- **Tags**: `nd`  `Semidefinite programming`  `non-projective measurements`  `quantum measurement certification`  `trapped ions`

### TL;DR

This paper introduces a complete SDP hierarchy to certify non-projective measurements and demonstrates its experimental applicability on a trapped-ion system.

### Motivation

Distinguishing projective from non-projective measurements is crucial for quantum information tasks that require non-projective measurements, but existing certification methods were incomplete or not robust.

### Method

They develop a complete semidefinite programming hierarchy that provides necessary and sufficient conditions for the existence of a projective simulation, and implement it in an experiment.

### Result

They certify non-projective measurements in a trapped-ion experiment, proving the impossibility of explaining the data with projective measurements.

### Conclusion

This provides a robust and complete framework for certifying non-projective measurements, with practical applications in quantum information processing.

*Keywords: Semidefinite programming, non-projective measurements, quantum measurement certification, trapped ions, device-independent*

- **Score**: 8.5 / 10  |  **Recommended**: ✅ Yes
- **Source**: Phys. Rev. Lett.  |  **Feed**: Physical Review Letters
- **Direction**: Quantum Information
- **Published**: 2026-08-26
- **Authors**: E. A. Ramirez Trino And M. A. Rajabpour
- **Link**: [http://link.aps.org/doi/10.1103/2frt-tdg9](http://link.aps.org/doi/10.1103/2frt-tdg9)
- **Tags**: `4`  `stabilizer entropy`  `Shannon-Rényi entropy`  `Gaussian states`  `quantum critical chains`

### TL;DR

This paper proves that for quadratic fermionic Gaussian states, the stabilizer Rényi entropy exactly equals the Shannon-Rényi entropy, establishing a direct correspondence between these measures in quantum critical chains.

### Motivation

To understand the relationship between stabilizer entropy, which measures nonstabilizerness, and Shannon-Rényi entropy, which measures structure, and to provide exact results for many-body systems.

### Method

By analyzing Gaussian states of quadratic fermions and leveraging their properties to derive an exact equivalence between the two entropy measures.

### Result

For any Gaussian eigenstate, the stabilizer Rényi entropy equals the Shannon-Rényi entropy, meaning the two quantities are exactly the same for these states.

### Conclusion

This equivalence provides a new tool for studying nonstabilizerness in fermionic systems and may aid in detecting phase transitions and universality in quantum critical chains.

*Keywords: stabilizer entropy, Shannon-Rényi entropy, Gaussian states, quantum critical chains, nonstabilizerness*

The commonly used Caldeira-Leggett master equation satisfies detailed balance, but it is unclear if other formulations do, and how entropy production relates to this. Understanding these properties is crucial for applying stochastic thermodynamics to open quantum systems.

### Result

They find that some formulations violate detailed balance, leading to differences in entropy production, and they clarify the conditions under which a consistent thermodynamic interpretation is possible.

### Conclusion

### TL;DR

This paper shows that local monitoring of a free-fermion chain leads to extensive entanglement growth with volume law scaling, explained by super-Poissonian quantum jump distributions and dark intervals.

### Motivation

Understanding how measurement-induced dynamics affect entanglement in many-body quantum systems is crucial for quantum information processing and foundational questions about quantum measurement.

### Method

The authors study a free-fermion chain with a single-site density monitoring, analyzing quantum jump trajectories and computing the entanglement entropy of the quantum state conditioned on the measurement record.

### Result

They find that the entanglement entropy grows to a stationary state with volume law scaling, in contrast to unitary dynamics and the no-click limit, and attribute this to bunching of quantum jumps followed by long dark intervals. Increasing the number of monitored sites leads to area law via the Zeno effect.

### Conclusion

The presence of dark intervals in the jump statistics is the key mechanism for extensive entanglement growth, providing insight into measurement-induced phase transitions and the role of monitoring in quantum systems.

They convert a logical-level Shor's algorithm circuit for factoring 21 into a surface code circuit and then to a physical-level circuit, considering realistic superconducting qubit parameters. They find that the CDS closed-loop latency must be within tens of microseconds, achievable by distributing decoding data across multiple decoders with fast communication. They demonstrate that near-term hardware with ~0.1% physical error rates and ~1000 qubits can successfully execute the circuit.

A catalyst of size polynomial in the desired error suffices for arbitrarily good approximations. This leads to constant T-depth circuits for multi-qubit Toffoli, adder, and quantum Fourier transform.

### Conclusion

The existence of catalytic constant-depth circuits implies that Clifford+T is a universal gate set with constant T-depth for many operations, potentially reducing the overhead of magic state distillation.

### Method

The authors analyze resource costs for implementing Hamiltonian simulation using two compilation families: one that serializes input circuits by eliminating all Clifford gates, and another that directly compiles Clifford+T to lattice surgery operations. ### Result

They show that the optimal scheme varies with the simulation algorithm; Trotterization benefits by orders of magnitude from direct Clifford+T compilation, while quantum signal processing may benefit from the other approach, indicating no one-size-fits-all compilation.

- **Link**: [https://quantum-journal.org/papers/q-2026-08-07-2185/](https://quantum-journal.org/papers/q-2026-08-07-2185/)
- **Tags**: `nd`  `qubit`  `noise`  `hamiltonian`  `algorithm`

### TL;DR

提出基于图稀疏化和分解的QAOA近似编译方案，显著减少trapped-ion模拟器的电路复杂度并提升噪声韧性，并提供首个可证明的改进保证。

### Motivation

QAOA编译通常需要大量操作，导致噪声累积；稀疏化和分解虽被用作启发式，但缺乏理论保证，本文旨在提供严谨的改进。

### Method

利用图稀疏化减少边数（保持割结构），以及图分解将加权图拆为少量未加权图，针对trapped-ion编译中的Pauli-X和全对Ising脉冲进行优化。

### Result

对于Max-Cut，在近似比损失ε下，最坏情况脉冲数从O(n^2)降至O(n log n)，Pauli-X翻转次数从O(n^2)降至O(n log n)，并在模拟dephasing噪声实验中验证了近似比改进。

### Conclusion

这些近似编译技术提供可证明的电路复杂度和噪声韧性改善，可广泛应用于各类量子计算硬件，提升实验可行性。

*Keywords: QAOA, graph sparsification, graph decomposition, trapped-ion quantum computing, noise reduction*

### TL;DR

This paper proves an unconditional separation between constant-depth quantum circuits and bounded-fan-in classical circuits for sampling tasks.

### Motivation

Following Bravyi, Gosset, and Koenig's separation for search problems, the authors ask whether a similar separation holds for sampling tasks without input dependence, aiming to extend quantum advantage results.

### Method

They introduce a specific distribution and construct a constant-depth quantum circuit sampling from it, then prove lower bounds on the depth of any classical circuit with bounded fan-in gates that approximates this distribution to within additive error.

### Result

They show that any classical bounded-fan-in circuit requiring a bounded amount of input randomness (with entropy r) must have depth at least Ω(log r) to sample from the distribution, while a constant-depth quantum circuit suffices, unconditionally.

### Conclusion

This provides an unconditional proof that constant-depth quantum circuits outperform constant-depth classical circuits in sampling tasks, contributing to quantum computational advantage.

*Keywords: quantum advantage, sampling problem, constant-depth circuits, classical circuit lower bound, total variation distance*

- **Link**: [http://link.aps.org/doi/10.1103/589s-s1yy](http://link.aps.org/doi/10.1103/589s-s1yy)
- **Tags**: `nd`  `altermagnet`  `anomalous Hall effect`  `uniaxial strain`  `Berry curvature`

### TL;DR

This paper studies the strain-tunable anomalous Hall effect in hexagonal MnTe, an altermagnet, and demonstrates that uniaxial strain can isolate a single magnetic domain and modify the Berry curvature, leading to a sharp anomalous Hall effect and sign reversal.

### Motivation

To understand and control the anomalous Hall effect in altermagnets, which are a new class of magnetic materials with potential for spintronic applications.

### Method

The authors applied uniaxial strain to single crystals of hexagonal MnTe and measured the Hall effect as a function of strain and temperature, along with neutron scattering to characterize the magnetic structure.

### Result

Uniaxial strain was found to isolate a single magnetic domain, resulting in a sharp anomalous Hall effect. The sign of the anomalous Hall effect could be reversed by tuning the strain, which was attributed to modifications in the electronic Berry curvature.

### TL;DR

This paper characterizes the computational complexity of simulating classical linear dynamics with geometrically local interactions on quantum computers, showing no exponential speedup for short-time dynamics but potential super-polynomial advantages for long-time dynamics under specific computational constraints.

### Motivation

To determine whether quantum computers can provide exponential speedup for simulating classical systems with local interactions, which are common in partial differential equations, unlike previous work that focused on long-range interactions.

### Method

The authors dequantize the quantum algorithm for short-time dynamics, prove that simulating short-time dynamics is at least as hard as polynomial-time and linear-space probabilistic classical computation, and analyze long-time dynamics complexity in terms of exponential-time and polynomial-space quantum computation.

### Result

Short-time dynamics simulation does not yield exponential quantum advantage, while long-time dynamics may offer super-polynomial time advantage under polynomial-space restriction or exponential space advantage otherwise.

### Conclusion

The work provides new insights into the computational complexity of classical dynamics governed by partial differential equations and identifies conditions where quantum advantage could be achieved.

## 317. The Paradox Of Steady-state Light Diffusion

- **Score**: 1.0 / 10  |  **Recommended**: ❌ No
- **Source**: Rep. Prog. Phys.  |  **Feed**: Reports on Progress in Physics (ROPP)
- **Direction**: General / Other
- **Published**: 2026-08-10
- **Authors**: André Liemert, Lorenzo Pattelli, Alwin Kienle, Federico Tommasi And Fabrizio Martelli
- **Link**: [https://iopscience.iop.org/article/10.1088/1361-6633/ae8eaa](https://iopscience.iop.org/article/10.1088/1361-6633/ae8eaa)
- **Tags**: `nd`  `diffusion`  `light diffusion`  `radiative transfer equation`  `diffusion coefficient`

### TL;DR

The paper resolves the controversy of absorption-dependent diffusion coefficients in light transport, proving that only in the time domain is the diffusion approximation self-consistent, while in continuous-wave regime no scalar diffusion coefficient can match the radiative transfer equation asymptotically.

### Motivation

The definition of the diffusion coefficient in light transport has been debated for decades, with absorption-dependent forms violating scaling, and the need to resolve the controversy.

### Method

The authors use an exact consistency test based on the convergence of diffusion to the radiative transfer equation in the limit of infinite propagation, analyzing both time-domain and continuous-wave regimes analytically.

### Result

In the time domain, the absorption-independent coefficient is necessary and sufficient for exact long-time convergence; in continuous-wave, no scalar diffusion coefficient can make diffusion asymptotically equivalent to the RTE when absorption is nonzero.

### Conclusion

The time-domain formulation is the only self-consistent reference, calling for re-examination of diffusion application in optical transport.

*Keywords: light diffusion, radiative transfer equation, diffusion coefficient, absorption, time-domain*

---

## 318. Probing Quark Electromagnetic Properties Via Entangled Quark Pairs In Fragmentation Hadrons At Lepton Colliders

- **Score**: 1.0 / 10  |  **Recommended**: ❌ No

- **Link**: [https://iopscience.iop.org/article/10.1088/1361-6633/ae8932](https://iopscience.iop.org/article/10.1088/1361-6633/ae8932)
- **Tags**: `nd`  `fragmentation`  `quark dipole moments`  `azimuthal asymmetries`  `lepton colliders`

### TL;DR

This paper proposes a new collider probe of light-quark electromagnetic dipole moments using entangled quark pairs and azimuthal asymmetries in dihadron production, constraining dipole couplings with Belle/BaBar data.

### Motivation

To provide a clean and novel method to probe light-quark dipole interactions, reducing dependence on fragmentation functions and avoiding contamination from other new physics effects.

### Method

The authors analyze spin correlations in quark pairs produced at lepton colliders, which lead to distinct azimuthal asymmetries in inclusive dihadron and back-to-back hadron pair production. They use published Belle and BaBar measurements and projected sensitivities.

### Result

The measurements provide significant constraints on light-quark dipole couplings, with reduced dependence on nonperturbative fragmentation functions and free from other new physics contamination.

### Motivation

To challenge the common assumption that rocky exoplanets always differentiate into a silicate mantle and an iron core, by investigating the miscibility of metal and silicate at extreme pressures and temperatures.

### Method

The authors used direct computational simulations to model the behavior of iron-silicate systems under the high-pressure, high-temperature conditions expected in exoplanet interiors.

### Result

They found that iron and silicate can become miscible, preventing core formation and resulting in coreless exoplanets.

### Conclusion

The study suggests that some exoplanets may lack a metallic core, which has implications for their interior structure, magnetic field generation, and evolution.

### Motivation

To directly detect dark matter particles and measure coherent elastic neutrino-nucleus scattering, a process that is both a background and a signal for dark matter searches.

### Method

Using the LUX-ZEPLIN (LZ) dual-phase xenon time projection chamber to observe nuclear recoils from solar neutrinos and search for light dark matter interactions.

### Result

Strong evidence (4.5σ) for coherent elastic neutrino-nucleus scattering from solar neutrinos, along with new limits on light dark matter cross-sections.

### Conclusion

The observation of CEνNS with solar neutrinos validates the detector's sensitivity and improves the understanding of backgrounds for future dark matter searches.

- **Direction**: General / Other
- **Published**: 2026-08-18
- **Authors**: Xinsong Guomengyan Liuqingcheng Xionghowai Ngaimingdong Hexinying Liyingwei Zhengfuqiang Xuminghong Maruiqi Wuashanghai Pudong Hospital, Fudan University Pudong Medical Center, State Key Laboratory Of Brain Function And Disorders, Ministry Of Education Frontiers Center For Brain Science, Institutes Of Brain Science, Fudan University, Shanghai 200032, Chinabhttps://ror.org/03ab0at74state Key Laboratory Of Digital Medical Engineering, Key Laboratory Of Biomedical Engineering Of Hainan Province, School Of Biomedical Engineering, Hainan University, Sanya 572025, Chinacjiangsu Key Laboratory Of Brain Disease And Bioinformation, School Of Basic Medicine Sciences, Xuzhou Medical University, Xuzhou, Jiangsu 221004, Chinadhttps://ror.org/034t30j35shenzhen Institutes Of Advanced Technology, Chinese Academy Of Sciences, Shenzhen, Guangdong 518055, Chinaehttps://ror.org/00b30xv10department Of Neuroscience, Perelman School Of Medicine, University Of Pennsylvania, Philadelphia, PA 19104
- **Link**: [https://www.pnas.org/doi/abs/10.1073/pnas.2603853123?af=R](https://www.pnas.org/doi/abs/10.1073/pnas.2603853123?af=R)
- **Tags**: `nd`  `4`  `disorder`  `nasal afferent`  `anxiety`

### TL;DR

This paper investigates a nose-to-brain neural circuit that regulates anxiety through nasal afferent frequency in mice, potentially offering a noninvasive therapeutic target for anxiety disorders.

### Motivation

Anxiety disorders are widespread and there is an urgent need for effective noninvasive treatments; understanding the neural basis of anxiety regulation could lead to new interventions.

### Method

The study likely uses mouse models with optogenetic or chemogenetic manipulation to alter nasal afferent frequency and observe anxiety-related behaviors.

### Result

They found that nasal afferent frequency causally and bidirectionally regulates anxiety, suggesting a specific neural circuit involved.

### Conclusion

The findings identify a novel nose-to-brain pathway for anxiety regulation, which could be targeted for noninvasive therapies.

*Keywords: nasal afferent, anxiety, neural circuit, mouse model, noninvasive therapy*

Meyerahttps://ror.org/02ets8c94department Of Pharmacology And Toxicology, Indiana University School Of Medicine, Indianapolis, IN 46202bhttps://ror.org/02ets8c94stark Neurosciences Research Institute, Indiana University School Of Medicine, Indianapolis, IN 46202chttps://ror.org/02ets8c94department Of Medical And Molecular Genetics, Indiana University School Of Medicine, Indianapolis, IN 46202dhttps://ror.org/01kg8sb98department Of Biology, Indiana University Purdue University Indianapolis, Indianapolis, IN 46202ehttps://ror.org/02ets8c94department Of Ophthalmology, Indiana University School Of Medicine, Indianapolis, IN 46202

- **Tags**: `nd`  `4`  `neuronal`  `human pluripotent stem cells`  `microglia`

### TL;DR

This paper describes a human stem cell triculture model to study how microglia regulate retinal ganglion cells during neuroinflammation.

### Motivation

There is a lack of physiologically relevant human models to study microglial contributions to retinal neurodegeneration.

### Method

The authors developed a triculture platform using human pluripotent stem cells to co-culture microglia and retinal ganglion cells.

### Result

The platform enables investigation of microglial regulation of retinal ganglion cells in a human-relevant in vitro system.

### Conclusion

This model can help elucidate neuroinflammatory mechanisms and may aid in understanding retinal neurodegeneration.

*Keywords: human pluripotent stem cells, microglia, retinal ganglion cells, neuroinflammation, triculture*
